The immediate trigger was noisy third-party output and an `aiohttp.ClientSession.__del__` warning during cancellation cleanup, but the observed deadlock appears to be a Rich lock-order inversion between `Live._lock` and `Console._lock`.\\n\\nI understand redirecting global stdout/stderr during live rendering is inherently tricky. If this usage pattern is unsupported, a docs warning would help. If it is expected to be safe, I think there is a real deadlock here.\\n\\n## Environment\\n\\n- Python: 3.13.3\\n- Rich: 14.2.0\\n- aiohttp: 3.13.4\\n- LiteLLM: 1.86.2\\n- Terminal: tmux pane\\n- Platform: Linux\\n\\n## What happened\\n\\nThe CLI froze permanently: the `Live` spinner stopped, no progress counters advanced, and no job files were written for 9+ hours. The process was still alive but all useful work had stopped.\\n\\nAt the time of the freeze:\\n\\n- 46 TLS sockets owned by the process were in `CLOSE-WAIT`.\\n- Many asyncio tasks had recently been cancelled by `asyncio.wait_for(...)` timeouts.\\n- `aiohttp.ClientSession.__del__` was calling the event loop exception handler for an unclosed session.\\n- Third-party code was also printing directly to stdout.\\n\\n## Observed stacks\\n\\nAbbreviated `py-spy dump` output from the wedged process:\\n\\n```text\\nThread 2525706 (idle): \\\"MainThread\\\"\\n    process_renderables (rich/live.py:281)\\n    print (rich/console.py:1708)\\n    write (rich/file_proxy.py:47)\\n    emit (logging/__init__.py:1153)\\n    handle (logging/__init__.py:1026)\\n    callHandlers (logging/__init__.py:1744)\\n    handle (logging/__init__.py:1680)\\n    _log (logging/__init__.py:1664)\\n    error (logging/__init__.py:1548)\\n    default_exception_handler (asyncio/base_events.py:1865)\\n    call_exception_handler (asyncio/base_events.py:1891)\\n    __del__ (aiohttp/client.py:465)\\n    ...\\n    print (rich/console.py:1724)\\n    write (rich/file_proxy.py:47)\\n    get_llm_provider (.../litellm/.../get_llm_provider_logic.py:503)\\n    completion_cost (.../litellm/cost_calculator.py:1255)\\n```\\n\\n```text\\nThread 2525755 (idle): \\\"Thread-1\\\"\\n    render_lines (rich/console.py:1375)\\n    __rich_console__ (rich/live_render.py:81)\\n    render (rich/console.py:1345)\\n    print (rich/console.py:1724)\\n    refresh (rich/live.py:267)\\n    run (rich/live.py:38)\\n```\\n\\nAnother worker thread was also in redirected stdout rendering:\\n\\n```text\\nThread 2526618 (idle): \\\"asyncio_27\\\"\\n    render_lines (rich/console.py:1375)\\n    __rich_console__ (rich/live_render.py:81)\\n    render (rich/console.py:1345)\\n    print (rich/console.py:1724)\\n    write (rich/file_proxy.py:47)\\n    ... third-party library print path ...\\n```\\n\\n## Suspected lock inversion\\n\\nFrom Rich 14.2.0 source:\\n\\n- `Live._RefreshThread.run()` enters `with self.live._lock:` and then calls `self.live.refresh()`.\\n- `Live.refresh()` calls `self.console.print(...)` while still inside the live lock path.\\n- `Console.print()` eventually needs `Console._lock` in rendering/writing paths.\\n- `FileProxy.write()` calls `console.print(...)` for redirected stdout/stderr.\\n- `Console.print()` invokes live render hooks; `Live.process_renderables()` takes `Live._lock`.\\n\\nThe observed deadlock looks like this:\\n\\n```text\\nmain thread:\\n  owns Console._lock\\n  -> FileProxy/logging path re-enters Rich\\n  -> waits for Live._lock in Live.process_renderables()\\n\\nrefresh thread:\\n  owns Live._lock\\n  -> calls console.print() from Live.refresh()\\n  -> waits for Console._lock\\n```\\n\\nSo the two threads wait forever:\\n\\n```text\\nmain thread:    Console._lock -> waits Live._lock\\nrefresh thread: Live._lock    -> waits Console._lock\\n```\\n\\nLinux syscall state supported this: the hot Rich threads were parked in futex waits, not blocked on network IO.\\n\\n## Why stderr redirection matters\\n\\n`aiohttp.ClientSession.__del__` calls `loop.call_exception_handler(...)`, and asyncio's default handler logs with Python logging. If no explicit handler captures it, Python's fallback stderr handler writes to the current `sys.stderr`. During `Live`, Rich has replaced `sys.stderr` with `FileProxy`, so finalizer/error logging can re-enter Rich at arbitrary points during a render.\\n\\nThird-party `print(...)` calls can do the same through redirected stdout.\\n\\n## Workaround\\n\\nFor our app, disabling stdout/stderr redirection appears to be the right defensive workaround:\\n\\n```python\\nwith Live(\\n    Group(loading_progress, running_progress),\\n    refresh_per_second=10,\\n    redirect_stdout=False,\\n    redirect_stderr=False,\\n):\\n    ...\\n```\\n\\nWe are also suppressing noisy third-party stdout output separately.\\n\\n## Question\\n\\nIs `Live(..., redirect_stdout=True, redirect_stderr=True, auto_refresh=True)` intended to be safe when arbitrary third-party threads/tasks may print or log to stdout/stderr during rendering?\\n\\nIf yes, I think the lock ordering in `Live` / `Console` / `FileProxy` can deadlock. If no, would you accept a docs warning around redirected stdout/stderr in multi-threaded or high-concurrency asyncio applications?\",\n  \"author_association\": \"NONE\",\n  \"created_at\": \"2026-06-05T16:21:40Z\",\n  \"labels\": []\n}","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

2026-10-25T05:11:59.3144172Z {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_11","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/bash -lc \"sed -n '1,240p' docs/source/live.rst\"","aggregated_output":".. _live:\n\nLive Display\n============\n\nProgress bars and status indicators use a *live* display to animate parts of the terminal. You can build custom live displays with the :class:`~rich.live.Live` class.\n\nFor a demonstration of a live display, run the following command::\n\n    python -m rich.live\n\n.. note::\n\n    If you see ellipsis \"...\", this indicates that the terminal is not tall enough to show the full table.\n\nBasic usage\n~~~~~~~~~~~\n\nTo create a live display, construct a :class:`~rich.live.Live` object with a renderable and use it as a context manager. The live display will persist for the duration of the context. You can update the renderable to update the display::\n\n\n    import time\n\n    from rich.live import Live\n    from rich.table import Table\n\n    table = Table()\n    table.add_column(\"Row ID\")\n    table.add_column(\"Description\")\n    table.add_column(\"Level\")\n\n    with Live(table, refresh_per_second=4):  # update 4 times a second to feel fluid\n        for row in range(12):\n            time.sleep(0.4)  # arbitrary delay\n            # update the renderable internally\n            table.add_row(f\"{row}\", f\"description {row}\", \"[red]ERROR\")\n\n\nUpdating the renderable\n~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\n\nYou can also change the renderable on-the-fly by calling the :meth:`~rich.live.Live.update` method. This may be useful if the information you wish to display is too dynamic to generate by updating a single renderable. Here is an example::\n\n    import random\n    import time\n\n    from rich.live import Live\n    from rich.table import Table\n\n\n    def generate_table() -> Table:\n        \"\"\"Make a new table.\"\"\"\n        table = Table()\n        table.add_column(\"ID\")\n        table.add_column(\"Value\")\n        table.add_column(\"Status\")\n\n        for row in range(random.randint(2, 6)):\n            value = random.random() * 100\n            table.add_row(\n                f\"{row}\", f\"{value:3.2f}\", \"[red]ERROR\" if value < 50 else \"[green]SUCCESS\"\n            )\n        return table\n\n\n    with Live(generate_table(), refresh_per_second=4) as live:\n        for _ in range(40):\n            time.sleep(0.4)\n            live.update(generate_table())\n\n\nAlternate screen\n~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\n\nYou can opt to show a Live display in the \"alternate screen\" by setting ``screen=True`` on the constructor. This will allow your live display to go full screen and restore the command prompt on exit.\n\nYou can use this feature in combination with :ref:`Layout` to display sophisticated terminal \"applications\".\n\nTransient display\n~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\n\nNormally when you exit live context manager (or call :meth:`~rich.live.Live.stop`) the last refreshed item remains in the terminal with the cursor on the following line.\nYou can also make the live display disappear on exit by setting ``transient=True`` on the Live constructor.\n\nAuto refresh\n~~~~~~~~~~~~\n\nBy default, the live display will refresh 4 times a second. You can set the refresh rate with the ``refresh_per_second`` argument on the :class:`~rich.live.Live` constructor.\nYou should set this to something lower than 4 if you know your updates will not be that frequent or higher for a smoother feeling.\n\nYou might want to disable auto-refresh entirely if your updates are not very frequent, which you can do by setting ``auto_refresh=False`` on the constructor.\nIf you disable auto-refresh you will need to call :meth:`~rich.live.Live.refresh` manually or :meth:`~rich.live.Live.update` with ``refresh=True``.\n\nVertical overflow\n~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\n\nBy default, the live display will display ellipsis if the renderable is too large for the terminal. You can adjust this by setting the\n``vertical_overflow`` argument on the :class:`~rich.live.Live` constructor.\n\n- \"crop\" Show renderable up to the terminal height. The rest is hidden.\n- \"ellipsis\" Similar to crop except last line of the terminal is replaced with \"...\". This is the default behavior.\n- \"visible\" Will allow the whole renderable to be shown. Note that the display cannot be properly cleared in this mode.\n\n.. note::\n\n    Once the live display stops on a non-transient renderable, the last frame will render as **visible** since it doesn't have to be cleared.\n\n\nPrint / log\n~~~~~~~~~~~\n\nThe Live class will create an internal Console object which you can access via ``live.console``. If you print or log to this console, the output will be displayed *above* the live display. Here's an example::\n\n    import time\n\n    from rich.live import Live\n    from rich.table import Table\n\n    table = Table()\n    table.add_column(\"Row ID\")\n    table.add_column(\"Description\")\n    table.add_column(\"Level\")\n\n    with Live(table, refresh_per_second=4) as live:  # update 4 times a second to feel fluid\n        for row in range(12):\n            live.console.print(f\"Working on row #{row}\")\n            time.sleep(0.4)\n            table.add_row(f\"{row}\", f\"description {row}\", \"[red]ERROR\")\n\n\nIf you have another Console object you want to use, pass it in to the :class:`~rich.live.Live` constructor. Here's an example::\n\n    from my_project import my_console\n\n    with Live(console=my_console) as live:\n        my_console.print(\"[bold blue]Starting work!\")\n        ...\n\n.. note::\n\n    If you are passing in a file console, the live display only show the last item once the live context is left.\n\nRedirecting stdout / stderr\n~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\n\nTo avoid breaking the live display visuals, Rich will redirect ``stdout`` and ``stderr`` so that you can use the builtin ``print`` statement.\nThis feature is enabled by default, but you can disable by setting ``redirect_stdout`` or ``redirect_stderr`` to ``False``.\n\nNesting Lives\n-------------\n\nIf you create a Live instance within the context of an existing Live instance, then the content of the inner Live will be displayed below the outer Live.\n\nPrior to version 14.0.0 this would have resulted in a :class:`~rich.errors.LiveError` exception.\n\n\nExamples\n--------\n\nSee `table_movie.py <https://github.com/willmcgugan/rich/blob/master/examples/table_movie.py>`_ and\n`top_lite_simulator.py <https://github.com/willmcgugan/rich/blob/master/examples/top_lite_simulator.py>`_\nfor deeper examples of live displaying.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}
